# Z80 Core Implementation Plan (sfemu sub-project D1)

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** a cycle-accurate Zilog Z80 in `crates/z80`, verified instruction by instruction against all 1,604 SingleStepTests/z80 vector files, with no audio chip and no sound.

**Architecture:** a new dependency-free crate modelled on `crates/m68k`: all state in one `Z80` struct, every access through a `Bus` trait that has memory *and* I/O ports. `testrunner` gains a streaming fetcher that converts 1.37 GB of upstream JSON into ~236 MB of compact binary, and a second suite harness that compares final registers, final RAM, T-state totals, per-T-state bus pins, and port transactions.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2021, `rust-version` 1.93, no runtime dependencies in `z80`. `testrunner` is dev-only and may shell out to `curl`.

## Global Constraints

- **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-08-z80-core-design.md`. Read it before starting.
- **Workspace root:** `/Users/macini/Documents/dev/sfemu`. Six sub-projects (A, B, C, E1, E2, E3) are complete; this is D1 of D1/D2/D3.
- **`z80` has ZERO runtime dependencies**, `no_std`-compatible in the same sense `m68k` is: no `println!`, no `std::time`, no threads, no file I/O, no allocation in the core.
- **Determinism:** no wall-clock, no randomness, no globals, no `static mut`. All CPU state lives in `Z80`, which derives `Clone` + `PartialEq` + `Debug`. This is the WASM/netplay constraint that binds A–D.
- **Never panic on guest input.** Any byte sequence must execute. The Z80 has no illegal-instruction trap: undefined opcodes have defined behaviour and the suite has a file for each. Panics are for internal invariant violations only.
- **This sub-project produces NO SOUND.** No YM2151, no OKI, no host audio, no mixing. D2 and D3 do that. No test here asserts anything about audio.
- **`machine` is not modified.** `Cps1` does not gain a Z80 in D1 — D2 wires it. `crates/z80` is standalone, exactly as `m68k` was in A.
- **Vector data:** `https://github.com/SingleStepTests/z80`, directory `v1/`, MIT. **1,604 files, 1,000 cases each = 1,604,000 cases.** Converted into `testdata/z80/`, which is **gitignored — never commit test data**.
- **Missing test data fails loudly**, naming the file and the fetch command. No `#[ignore]`, no env-var escape hatch, no silent skip. This rule has no exemption for diagnostics or fixtures.
- **No ROM is ever downloaded, bundled, or committed.** Nothing in D1 needs one.
- **The suite is ground truth.** Debug from suite failure diffs, not from standalone analysis harnesses.
- **A test must not derive its expected value from the thing under test.** This branch's characteristic defect; it has been found and fixed six times. Hand-computed flag expectations are written from the Zilog manual, never read off the core.
- **Commit after every task.** Before each commit: `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo test --workspace --release`, `cargo doc --no-deps --workspace`. All clean.
- **The 68000 suite must stay at 127/127, 317,500/317,500.** Verify with `cargo run -q -p testrunner --release --bin report -- --test suite` if anything shared is touched. D1 should touch nothing shared, so movement is a regression to investigate, never a tolerance.
- **Never lower a target or add to a skip list.** A surviving KILL-expected mutant means write the missing test. A failing group means fix the core or document upstream-known-bad with evidence.

---

## Performance: the spec's risk is retired

The spec listed "1.6 M cases at per-T-state granularity is slow" as a risk and
suggested per-file parallelism as the fallback. **Measured before planning: the
68000 suite runs 317,500 cases and reads 132 MB in 0.48 s wall, single-threaded,
three runs identical** — about 660,000 cases/second.

Scaling by case count puts 1,604,000 Z80 cases near **3 s**, and the T-state
comparison is a handful of integer compares per cycle against a `Vec` the parser
already produced. So:

- **Do not add `rayon` or threads.** The constraint against dependencies outranks a
  saving on an already-fast suite, and thread-ordered output would make failures
  harder to read.
- Do not add a `--quick` subset mode. Every case runs every time.
- If the suite does exceed ~60 s in release, that is a real finding: report it
  rather than working around it, because at that point something is quadratic.

## File Structure

| File | Responsibility |
|------|----------------|
| `Cargo.toml` | Workspace manifest — add `crates/z80` to `members` |
| `crates/z80/Cargo.toml` | Crate manifest; optional off-by-default `serde` feature, mirroring `m68k` |
| `crates/z80/src/lib.rs` | Crate root, re-exports, `no_std` attrs, the `deny(rustdoc::private_intra_doc_links)` lint |
| `crates/z80/src/bus.rs` | `Bus` trait: `read`/`write`/`port_in`/`port_out` |
| `crates/z80/src/cpu.rs` | `Z80` struct: registers, shadow set, `WZ`, `Q`, `P`, IFF1/IFF2, IM, `reset`, `step` |
| `crates/z80/src/flags.rs` | Flag rules: S Z F5 H F3 P/V N C, the `Q` protocol, parity, `DAA` |
| `crates/z80/src/decode.rs` | The five pages (base, `CB`, `ED`, `DD`/`FD`, `DD CB`/`FD CB`) and dispatch |
| `crates/z80/src/ops/mod.rs` | Handler module tree |
| `crates/z80/src/ops/load.rs` | 8/16-bit loads, `EX`, `EXX`, `PUSH`/`POP`, `LDI`/`LDD`/`LDIR`/`LDDR`, `CPI` family |
| `crates/z80/src/ops/alu.rs` | `ADD`/`ADC`/`SUB`/`SBC`/`AND`/`OR`/`XOR`/`CP`/`INC`/`DEC`/`DAA`/`NEG`/`CPL`/16-bit `ADD`/`ADC`/`SBC` |
| `crates/z80/src/ops/bits.rs` | The `CB` page: `RLC`/`RRC`/`RL`/`RR`/`SLA`/`SRA`/`SLL`/`SRL`, `BIT`/`SET`/`RES`, `RLD`/`RRD` |
| `crates/z80/src/ops/flow.rs` | `JP`/`JR`/`CALL`/`RET`/`RETI`/`RETN`/`DJNZ`/`RST`, conditions, `HALT`, `EI`/`DI`, `IM` |
| `crates/z80/src/ops/io.rs` | `IN`/`OUT` and the `ED` block-I/O forms `INI`/`IND`/`INIR`/`INDR`/`OUTI`/`OTIR`/`OUTD`/`OTDR` |
| `crates/z80/src/interrupt.rs` | NMI and IM 0/1/2 acceptance, the `EI`-delay rule |
| `crates/z80/src/disasm.rs` | One-instruction disassembly, for D2's debugger panel |
| `crates/testrunner/src/z80fmt.rs` | The `Z80V` binary format: writer (used by fetch) and parser (used by the suite) |
| `crates/testrunner/src/z80json.rs` | Minimal JSON reader for the one upstream shape — dev-only, no serde |
| `crates/testrunner/src/z80bus.rs` | Recording `Bus` impl: seeded RAM, T-state trace, port log |
| `crates/testrunner/src/z80runner.rs` | Run one case, diff state / RAM / T-states / pins / ports, report readably |
| `crates/testrunner/src/bin/fetchz80.rs` | Streaming fetch: download JSON → convert → delete → next |
| `crates/testrunner/src/bin/reportz80.rs` | Full-suite report, one row per file, then the tally |
| `crates/testrunner/tests/z80suite.rs` | One `#[test]` per **page group**, plus the completeness test |
| `scripts/mutate.py` | Two new mutant sets: `z80flags` and `z80int` |
| `README.md` | D1's row, the crate, and what still makes no sound |

### Why the suite tests group by page, not by file

The 68000 harness has one `#[test]` per file and a hand-written `groups!` macro
listing all 127. **1,604 hand-written entries is not a plan, it is a liability** —
and the completeness test that made the 127-entry list worth keeping can be
satisfied a better way here.

So `z80suite.rs` has **11 `#[test]`s** covering the seven pages, split where a page
is large enough that a failure name should narrow it:

| test | files | what it covers |
|------|-------|----------------|
| `base_00_7f` | 128 | loads, `HALT`, register ALU |
| `base_80_ff` | 124 | ALU, flow, `RST`, prefix-adjacent |
| `cb_page` | 256 | rotates, shifts, `BIT`/`SET`/`RES`, `SLL` |
| `ed_page` | 80 | block moves, block I/O, `IM`, `RETN`, `RLD` |
| `dd_page` | 252 | `IX` forms and unprefixed fallbacks |
| `fd_page` | 252 | `IY` forms and unprefixed fallbacks |
| `ddcb_page` | 256 | the undocumented `DD CB` double-prefix page |
| `fdcb_page` | 256 | the undocumented `FD CB` double-prefix page |
| `every_vector_file_is_covered` | — | the completeness test: walks `testdata/z80/`, asserts every `.z80bin` is claimed by exactly one page predicate |
| `every_page_has_its_expected_file_count` | — | the counts in the table above, as literals |
| `no_page_is_empty` | — | each page test would pass vacuously on an empty directory; this forbids it |

The last three are the substance. A page test that walked a directory and found
nothing would report success, which is the exact vacuous-pass shape this project
has caught repeatedly — so file counts are pinned as **literals** (252/256/80/…),
derived from the upstream inventory and not from `read_dir().count()`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the crate manifest**

`crates/z80/Cargo.toml`, mirroring `crates/m68k/Cargo.toml`'s feature shape:

```toml
[package]
name = "z80"
version = "0.1.0"
edition.workspace = true
rust-version.workspace = true
publish = false
description = "Zilog Z80 CPU core"

# Zero runtime dependencies, like `m68k`. `serde` is optional and off by default
# so a save state can serialize the core in D2 without the emulator carrying
# serde in the WASM path.
[dependencies]
serde = { version = "1", optional = true, default-features = false, features = ["derive"] }

[features]
default = ["std"]
std = []
serde = ["dep:serde"]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the failing test for register pairs and reset**

Create `crates/z80/src/cpu.rs` with the test module first. These expectations come
from the Zilog *Z80 CPU User Manual* and from the suite's state-block field list —
**not** from running the core:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// The 16-bit pairs are the 8-bit registers, high byte first.
    ///
    /// Hand-written from the architecture, not read back from a setter: `B` is the
    /// high half of `BC` on a Z80, and a core that swapped them would still be
    /// self-consistent under `set_bc(bc())` — which is why that identity is not
    /// what is asserted here.
    #[test]
    fn a_register_pair_is_its_two_halves_high_byte_first() {
        let mut c = Z80::new();
        c.b = 0x12;
        c.c = 0x34;
        assert_eq!(c.bc(), 0x1234);
        c.d = 0xAB;
        c.e = 0xCD;
        assert_eq!(c.de(), 0xABCD);
        c.h = 0x00;
        c.l = 0xFF;
        assert_eq!(c.hl(), 0x00FF);
        c.a = 0x7F;
        c.f = 0x80;
        assert_eq!(c.af(), 0x7F80, "A is the high half of AF, F the low");

        // And the setters land in the halves, in the same order.
        c.set_bc(0xDEAD);
        assert_eq!((c.b, c.c), (0xDE, 0xAD));
        c.set_hl(0xBEEF);
        assert_eq!((c.h, c.l), (0xBE, 0xEF));
        c.set_af(0x1234);
        assert_eq!((c.a, c.f), (0x12, 0x34));
    }

    /// Reset, per the Zilog manual: PC and I and R cleared, interrupts disabled,
    /// interrupt mode 0, and **AF and SP set to 0xFFFF**.
    ///
    /// The 0xFFFF is the detail worth a test: it is not zero, and a core that
    /// zeroed everything would boot differently on the first `PUSH`.
    #[test]
    fn reset_clears_the_program_counter_and_leaves_af_and_sp_all_ones() {
        let mut c = Z80::new();
        c.pc = 0x1234;
        c.i = 0x55;
        c.r = 0x7F;
        c.iff1 = true;
        c.iff2 = true;
        c.im = 2;
        c.reset();
        assert_eq!(c.pc, 0, "PC clear");
        assert_eq!(c.i, 0, "I clear");
        assert_eq!(c.r, 0, "R clear");
        assert!(!c.iff1, "interrupts disabled");
        assert!(!c.iff2);
        assert_eq!(c.im, 0, "interrupt mode 0");
        assert_eq!(c.af(), 0xFFFF, "AF is all ones after reset, not zero");
        assert_eq!(c.sp, 0xFFFF, "and so is SP");
    }

    /// `Q` and `P` are real state, not derived.
    ///
    /// Both are fields of the suite's state block, so they are compared on every
    /// one of 1,604,000 cases. `Q` records whether the last instruction wrote the
    /// flags (which `SCF`/`CCF` need for F3/F5) and `P` whether `LD A,I`/`LD A,R`
    /// was last. A core without them cannot pass `37.json` (`SCF`).
    #[test]
    fn the_undocumented_state_exists_and_starts_clear() {
        let c = Z80::new();
        assert_eq!(c.q, 0, "no instruction has run, so no flags were written");
        assert_eq!(c.p, 0);
        assert_eq!(c.wz, 0, "the internal address latch");
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `bus.rs`**

```rust
//! The interface between the CPU and everything else.

/// The CPU's view of the outside world.
///
/// Four methods rather than two, because the Z80 has a **separate 16-bit I/O
/// address space** and the vectors verify it as a distinct pin state (`r--i` and
/// `-w-i`, against `r-m-` and `-wm-` for memory). A core that routed ports
/// through `read`/`write` would pass every register comparison and fail every
/// bus-trace comparison on the I/O pages.
///
/// There is deliberately no `read16`: the Z80's data bus is 8 bits, and every
/// 16-bit access is two byte accesses in a defined order. Composing them in the
/// core is what makes the T-state counts fall out instead of needing a table —
/// the same reasoning `m68k::Bus` records for its missing `read32`.
pub trait Bus {
    fn read(&mut self, addr: u16) -> u8;
    fn write(&mut self, addr: u16, val: u8);
    /// `port` is the **full 16 bits**, not the low 8.
    ///
    /// `IN A,(n)` puts `A` on the high half and `IN r,(C)` puts `B` there, so a
    /// core that masked to 8 bits would pass the common cases and fail those two.
    /// The suite's `ports` array records 16 bits for exactly this reason.
    fn port_in(&mut self, port: u16) -> u8;
    fn port_out(&mut self, port: u16, val: u8);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write `cpu.rs`'s state and `reset`**

Above the test module written in Step 3:

```rust
//! The Z80's registers and its step entry point.

use crate::Bus;

/// A Z80 CPU.
///
/// Every field the vector suite's state block carries, and nothing else. The
/// suite compares 26 fields on each of 1,604,000 cases, so a field that exists
/// here but not there is unverified and a field there but not here is a test
/// that cannot pass.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Debug, Default)]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize))]
pub struct Z80 {
    pub a: u8,
    pub f: u8,
    pub b: u8,
    pub c: u8,
    pub d: u8,
    pub e: u8,
    pub h: u8,
    pub l: u8,
    /// Interrupt vector base, the high byte of an IM 2 vector.
    pub i: u8,
    /// Memory refresh counter. Bit 7 is preserved across increments; the low 7
    /// bits advance once per M1 cycle, which is observable via `LD A,R`.
    pub r: u8,
    pub ix: u16,
    pub iy: u16,
    pub sp: u16,
    pub pc: u16,
    /// The internal address latch.
    ///
    /// Not architecturally visible and absent from most documentation, but it
    /// drives the address pins during some T-states, so the suite compares it.
    pub wz: u16,
    /// The shadow registers, as packed pairs — `EX AF,AF'` and `EXX` swap these
    /// wholesale, so there is no reason to hold eight more bytes.
    pub af_: u16,
    pub bc_: u16,
    pub de_: u16,
    pub hl_: u16,
    pub iff1: bool,
    pub iff2: bool,
    /// Interrupt mode: 0, 1, or 2.
    pub im: u8,
    /// Set when `EI` executed and the enable has not yet taken effect.
    ///
    /// `EI` does not enable interrupts until *after* the following instruction,
    /// so an `EI`/`RET` pair cannot be interrupted between them. This is that
    /// one-instruction delay, and the suite carries it as a state field.
    pub ei: u8,
    /// Whether the last instruction wrote the flags.
    ///
    /// `SCF` and `CCF` compute F3/F5 from `A` **or** from the previous flags
    /// depending on this. It is the most commonly omitted piece of Z80 state and
    /// the suite compares it on every case.
    pub q: u8,
    /// Whether the last instruction was `LD A,I` or `LD A,R`.
    pub p: u8,
}

impl Z80 {
    /// A CPU in its power-on state.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        let mut c = Self::default();
        c.reset();
        c
    }

    /// Reset, per the Zilog manual: PC, I and R cleared, interrupts disabled,
    /// mode 0, and **AF and SP all ones** — not zero.
    pub fn reset(&mut self) {
        self.pc = 0;
        self.i = 0;
        self.r = 0;
        self.iff1 = false;
        self.iff2 = false;
        self.im = 0;
        self.ei = 0;
        self.q = 0;
        self.p = 0;
        self.wz = 0;
        self.set_af(0xFFFF);
        self.sp = 0xFFFF;
    }

    /// `BC` as one 16-bit value: `B` high, `C` low.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn bc(&self) -> u16 {
        u16::from(self.b) << 8 | u16::from(self.c)
    }
    /// `DE`: `D` high, `E` low.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn de(&self) -> u16 {
        u16::from(self.d) << 8 | u16::from(self.e)
    }
    /// `HL`: `H` high, `L` low.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn hl(&self) -> u16 {
        u16::from(self.h) << 8 | u16::from(self.l)
    }
    /// `AF`: `A` high, `F` low.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn af(&self) -> u16 {
        u16::from(self.a) << 8 | u16::from(self.f)
    }

    /// Splits `v` into `B` and `C`.
    pub fn set_bc(&mut self, v: u16) {
        self.b = (v >> 8) as u8;
        self.c = v as u8;
    }
    /// Splits `v` into `D` and `E`.
    pub fn set_de(&mut self, v: u16) {
        self.d = (v >> 8) as u8;
        self.e = v as u8;
    }
    /// Splits `v` into `H` and `L`.
    pub fn set_hl(&mut self, v: u16) {
        self.h = (v >> 8) as u8;
        self.l = v as u8;
    }
    /// Splits `v` into `A` and `F`.
    pub fn set_af(&mut self, v: u16) {
        self.a = (v >> 8) as u8;
        self.f = v as u8;
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Write `lib.rs`**

```rust
//! A Zilog Z80 CPU core.
//!
//! The core is free of dependencies, host I/O, and clock access: all state lives
//! in [`Z80`], and every access goes through [`Bus`]. That keeps it
//! deterministic, snapshot-able, and usable from WASM — the same constraint
//! `m68k` honours.
//!
//! # Why this core has cycle costs and `m68k` does not
//!
//! `m68k` has no cycle table because the 68000 obeys a measured law: every bus
//! access is four cycles, so `cycles = 4 * accesses + idle`. **The Z80 obeys no
//! such law.** An opcode fetch is 4 T-states, a memory access 3, and the
//! internal cycles are per-instruction. So each handler here returns its own
//! T-state count, and those counts are taken from the vector suite rather than
//! from a table someone typed — every one of 1,604 files carries a `cycles`
//! array that is the authority.
//!
//! # No sound here
//!
//! This is sub-project D1. It emulates the CPU that *will* drive the YM2151 and
//! the OKI sample player, and it makes no sound of its own. D2 wires it to the
//! board; D3 produces audio.

#![cfg_attr(all(not(test), not(feature = "std")), no_std)]
#![forbid(unsafe_code)]
// A public module's docs linking to a private item renders as a dead link and
// clippy cannot see it — four such bugs survived thirteen tasks in `m68k` and
// were each found only by someone running `cargo doc`. Denying it fails the
// build instead.
#![deny(rustdoc::private_intra_doc_links)]

pub mod bus;
pub mod cpu;

pub use bus::Bus;
pub use cpu::Z80;
```

## Task 2: The `Z80V` binary format — writer and parser

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/testrunner/src/z80fmt.rs`
- Modify: `crates/testrunner/src/lib.rs` (add `pub mod z80fmt;`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from Task 1.
- Produces: `z80fmt::{Case, State, Cycle, Port, RamEntry, write_file, parse_file, ParseError}`,
  and `z80fmt::MAGIC`. Task 3's converter calls `write_file`; Task 5's runner
  calls `parse_file`.

**Format, verbatim from the spec** (little-endian throughout):

```
file:   u32 magic 0x5630_385A             u32 num_cases
case:   state initial, state final, u16 num_cycles, cycle[num_cycles],
        u8 num_ports, port[num_ports]
state:  u16 pc sp ix iy wz af_ bc_ de_ hl_       (9 x u16)
        u8  a b c d e f h l i r                  (10 x u8)
        u8  ei im p q iff1 iff2                  (6 x u8)
        u8  num_ram, then num_ram x { u16 addr, u8 val }
cycle:  u16 addr, u8 data, u8 flags
        flags bit0 data_valid, bit1 rd, bit2 wr, bit3 mreq, bit4 ioreq
port:   u16 addr, u8 val, u8 dir (0 = in, 1 = out)
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing round-trip and magic tests**

Create `crates/testrunner/src/z80fmt.rs` with its tests first:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// The magic's four bytes read `Z80V` in **file order**.
    ///
    /// Pinned as bytes, not as the `u32`: `'Z','8','0','V'` as a little-endian
    /// `u32` is `0x5630385A`, and writing the digits in the order they are read
    /// gives `0x5A383056` — a value that looks right in a spec and fails at
    /// runtime. The spec records this reversal as a caught mistake.
    #[test]
    fn the_magic_reads_z80v_in_file_order() {
        assert_eq!(MAGIC.to_le_bytes(), *b"Z80V");
        assert_eq!(MAGIC, 0x5630_385A);
    }

    /// A file written then parsed is the same cases.
    ///
    /// The case is built by hand with every optional part populated — RAM in both
    /// halves, several cycles with mixed validity, and a port — because a
    /// round-trip over an empty case would pass with half the writer missing.
    #[test]
    fn a_written_file_parses_back_to_the_same_cases() {
        let c = Case {
            initial: State {
                pc: 0x1234, sp: 0xFFFE, ix: 0x1111, iy: 0x2222, wz: 0x3333,
                af_: 0x4444, bc_: 0x5555, de_: 0x6666, hl_: 0x7777,
                a: 1, b: 2, c: 3, d: 4, e: 5, f: 6, h: 7, l: 8, i: 9, r: 10,
                ei: 1, im: 2, p: 1, q: 1, iff1: true, iff2: false,
                ram: vec![RamEntry { addr: 0x1234, val: 0xED }],
            },
            final_: State {
                pc: 0x1236, sp: 0xFFFE, ix: 0x1111, iy: 0x2222, wz: 0x9999,
                af_: 0x4444, bc_: 0x5555, de_: 0x6666, hl_: 0x7777,
                a: 0xFF, b: 2, c: 3, d: 4, e: 5, f: 0x42, h: 7, l: 8, i: 9, r: 11,
                ei: 0, im: 2, p: 0, q: 1, iff1: true, iff2: false,
                ram: vec![
                    RamEntry { addr: 0x1234, val: 0xED },
                    RamEntry { addr: 0x5678, val: 0x99 },
                ],
            },
            cycles: vec![
                Cycle { addr: 0x1234, data: None, rd: false, wr: false, mreq: false, ioreq: false },
                Cycle { addr: 0x1234, data: None, rd: true, wr: false, mreq: true, ioreq: false },
                Cycle { addr: 0x1234, data: Some(0xED), rd: false, wr: false, mreq: false, ioreq: false },
                Cycle { addr: 0x5678, data: Some(0x99), rd: false, wr: true, mreq: true, ioreq: false },
            ],
            ports: vec![Port { addr: 0xBEEF, val: 0x5A, out: true }],
        };
        let bytes = write_file(&[c.clone()]);
        let back = parse_file(&bytes).expect("round trip");
        assert_eq!(back.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(back[0], c);
    }

    /// A file whose magic is wrong is an error naming both values.
    #[test]
    fn a_bad_magic_is_an_error_naming_what_was_expected() {
        let mut bytes = write_file(&[]);
        bytes[0] ^= 0xFF;
        let e = parse_file(&bytes).expect_err("must reject");
        let msg = e.to_string();
        assert!(msg.contains("magic"), "{msg}");
        assert!(msg.contains("5630385A") || msg.contains("5630_385A"), "{msg}");
    }

    /// A truncated file is an error, not a panic and not a short read.
    ///
    /// Every prefix is tried, because a parser that checked only its own first
    /// field would pass a test that truncated only the header.
    #[test]
    fn every_truncation_is_an_error_rather_than_a_panic() {
        let full = write_file(&[Case::default()]);
        for n in 0..full.len() {
            let r = parse_file(&full[..n]);
            assert!(r.is_err(), "prefix of {n} bytes must not parse");
        }
        assert!(parse_file(&full).is_ok(), "and the whole file must");
    }

    /// `data_valid` is a bit of its own, not inferred from the pins.
    ///
    /// Measured upstream: `r-m-` samples carry **no** data (15,000 of 15,000) and
    /// `----` samples carry data about a quarter of the time. So a reader that
    /// derived validity from "is this a read cycle" would drop every byte the CPU
    /// returned. This asserts the two cases that shape the flag: an internal
    /// cycle *with* data, and a read request *without*.
    #[test]
    fn data_validity_is_independent_of_the_pins() {
        let c = Case {
            cycles: vec![
                Cycle { addr: 0x100, data: Some(0x42), rd: false, wr: false, mreq: false, ioreq: false },
                Cycle { addr: 0x100, data: None, rd: true, wr: false, mreq: true, ioreq: false },
            ],
            ..Case::default()
        };
        let back = parse_file(&write_file(&[c])).expect("round trip");
        assert_eq!(back[0].cycles[0].data, Some(0x42), "an internal cycle can carry data");
        assert!(!back[0].cycles[0].rd, "and still not be a read");
        assert_eq!(back[0].cycles[1].data, None, "a read request carries none");
        assert!(back[0].cycles[1].rd, "and is still a read");
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the types and the writer**

```rust
//! The `Z80V` vector format: a compact binary rendering of the
//! SingleStepTests/z80 JSON.
//!
//! Upstream ships JSON only — 1.37 GB of it, against 2.9 GiB free on the
//! development machine — and no crate in this workspace has a JSON parser,
//! deliberately. So `fetchz80` converts each file as it downloads and this module
//! defines what it writes: little-endian, a magic, a count, then fixed records.
//! Measured shrink is 5.8x across all seven opcode pages, so the whole suite is
//! about 236 MB.
//!
//! Case **names are dropped**. Upstream's name is `"<PAGE> <OP> <index>"`, fully
//! recoverable from the filename and the case index, and storing 1.6 M of them
//! would be 30 MB of nothing.

/// `Z80V` in file order. As a little-endian `u32` that is `0x5630_385A` — the
/// digits in reading order would be `0x5A38_3056`, which is a different file.
pub const MAGIC: u32 = 0x5630_385A;

/// One RAM location a case seeds or checks.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug, Default)]
pub struct RamEntry {
    pub addr: u16,
    pub val: u8,
}

/// A CPU state: the suite's 26 fields, plus the RAM the case declares.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Debug, Default)]
pub struct State {
    pub pc: u16, pub sp: u16, pub ix: u16, pub iy: u16, pub wz: u16,
    pub af_: u16, pub bc_: u16, pub de_: u16, pub hl_: u16,
    pub a: u8, pub b: u8, pub c: u8, pub d: u8, pub e: u8,
    pub f: u8, pub h: u8, pub l: u8, pub i: u8, pub r: u8,
    pub ei: u8, pub im: u8, pub p: u8, pub q: u8,
    pub iff1: bool, pub iff2: bool,
    pub ram: Vec<RamEntry>,
}

/// One bus sample, taken **between** T-states.
///
/// `data` is `None` when the bus is electrically disconnected from the CPU. That
/// is not derivable from the pins: a read's request T-state has `rd`/`mreq` set
/// and no data, and the byte appears on a later sample with no pins set at all.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug, Default)]
pub struct Cycle {
    pub addr: u16,
    pub data: Option<u8>,
    pub rd: bool,
    pub wr: bool,
    pub mreq: bool,
    pub ioreq: bool,
}

/// One I/O-space transaction. Present only on the `IN`/`OUT` pages.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug, Default)]
pub struct Port {
    pub addr: u16,
    pub val: u8,
    /// `true` for `OUT`, `false` for `IN`.
    pub out: bool,
}

/// One test case.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Debug, Default)]
pub struct Case {
    pub initial: State,
    pub final_: State,
    pub cycles: Vec<Cycle>,
    pub ports: Vec<Port>,
}

/// Serializes cases into the format this module documents.
///
/// # Panics
///
/// If a count exceeds its field: more than 255 RAM entries or ports, or more
/// than 65,535 cycles. Measured maxima upstream are 5, 1 and 23, so these
/// cannot fire on today's data — they exist because a silent `as u8` is how a
/// format grows a truncation bug when someone regenerates the suite with
/// different options. A checked bound can be raised safely; a truncating one
/// cannot.
#[must_use]
pub fn write_file(cases: &[Case]) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut o = Vec::new();
    o.extend_from_slice(&MAGIC.to_le_bytes());
    o.extend_from_slice(&u32::try_from(cases.len()).expect("case count fits u32").to_le_bytes());
    for c in cases {
        write_state(&mut o, &c.initial);
        write_state(&mut o, &c.final_);
        let n = u16::try_from(c.cycles.len()).expect("cycle count fits u16");
        o.extend_from_slice(&n.to_le_bytes());
        for cy in &c.cycles {
            o.extend_from_slice(&cy.addr.to_le_bytes());
            o.push(cy.data.unwrap_or(0));
            let mut f = 0u8;
            if cy.data.is_some() { f |= 1 }
            if cy.rd { f |= 2 }
            if cy.wr { f |= 4 }
            if cy.mreq { f |= 8 }
            if cy.ioreq { f |= 16 }
            o.push(f);
        }
        o.push(u8::try_from(c.ports.len()).expect("port count fits u8"));
        for p in &c.ports {
            o.extend_from_slice(&p.addr.to_le_bytes());
            o.push(p.val);
            o.push(u8::from(p.out));
        }
    }
    o
}

fn write_state(o: &mut Vec<u8>, s: &State) {
    for v in [s.pc, s.sp, s.ix, s.iy, s.wz, s.af_, s.bc_, s.de_, s.hl_] {
        o.extend_from_slice(&v.to_le_bytes());
    }
    o.extend_from_slice(&[s.a, s.b, s.c, s.d, s.e, s.f, s.h, s.l, s.i, s.r]);
    o.extend_from_slice(&[s.ei, s.im, s.p, s.q, u8::from(s.iff1), u8::from(s.iff2)]);
    o.push(u8::try_from(s.ram.len()).expect("ram count fits u8"));
    for r in &s.ram {
        o.extend_from_slice(&r.addr.to_le_bytes());
        o.push(r.val);
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the parser**

Append to `z80fmt.rs`:

```rust
/// What can be wrong with a vector file.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum ParseError {
    BadMagic { want: u32, got: u32 },
    Truncated { at: usize, need: usize },
}

impl core::fmt::Display for ParseError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut core::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> core::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            ParseError::BadMagic { want, got } => {
                write!(f, "bad magic: want {want:08X}, got {got:08X}")
            }
            ParseError::Truncated { at, need } => {
                write!(f, "truncated at byte {at}: need {need} more")
            }
        }
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for ParseError {}

/// A cursor that cannot read past its end.
struct Rd<'a> {
    b: &'a [u8],
    at: usize,
}

impl<'a> Rd<'a> {
    fn take(&mut self, n: usize) -> Result<&'a [u8], ParseError> {
        let end = self.at.checked_add(n).ok_or(ParseError::Truncated { at: self.at, need: n })?;
        if end > self.b.len() {
            return Err(ParseError::Truncated { at: self.at, need: end - self.b.len() });
        }
        let s = &self.b[self.at..end];
        self.at = end;
        Ok(s)
    }
    fn u8(&mut self) -> Result<u8, ParseError> {
        Ok(self.take(1)?[0])
    }
    fn u16(&mut self) -> Result<u16, ParseError> {
        let s = self.take(2)?;
        Ok(u16::from_le_bytes([s[0], s[1]]))
    }
    fn u32(&mut self) -> Result<u32, ParseError> {
        let s = self.take(4)?;
        Ok(u32::from_le_bytes([s[0], s[1], s[2], s[3]]))
    }
}

/// Parses a whole vector file.
pub fn parse_file(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<Case>, ParseError> {
    let mut r = Rd { b: bytes, at: 0 };
    let magic = r.u32()?;
    if magic != MAGIC {
        return Err(ParseError::BadMagic { want: MAGIC, got: magic });
    }
    let n = r.u32()? as usize;
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(n);
    for _ in 0..n {
        let initial = read_state(&mut r)?;
        let final_ = read_state(&mut r)?;
        let ncyc = r.u16()? as usize;
        let mut cycles = Vec::with_capacity(ncyc);
        for _ in 0..ncyc {
            let addr = r.u16()?;
            let data = r.u8()?;
            let f = r.u8()?;
            cycles.push(Cycle {
                addr,
                data: if f & 1 != 0 { Some(data) } else { None },
                rd: f & 2 != 0,
                wr: f & 4 != 0,
                mreq: f & 8 != 0,
                ioreq: f & 16 != 0,
            });
        }
        let nport = r.u8()? as usize;
        let mut ports = Vec::with_capacity(nport);
        for _ in 0..nport {
            ports.push(Port { addr: r.u16()?, val: r.u8()?, out: r.u8()? != 0 });
        }
        out.push(Case { initial, final_, cycles, ports });
    }
    Ok(out)
}

fn read_state(r: &mut Rd) -> Result<State, ParseError> {
    let mut s = State {
        pc: r.u16()?, sp: r.u16()?, ix: r.u16()?, iy: r.u16()?, wz: r.u16()?,
        af_: r.u16()?, bc_: r.u16()?, de_: r.u16()?, hl_: r.u16()?,
        ..State::default()
    };
    s.a = r.u8()?; s.b = r.u8()?; s.c = r.u8()?; s.d = r.u8()?; s.e = r.u8()?;
    s.f = r.u8()?; s.h = r.u8()?; s.l = r.u8()?; s.i = r.u8()?; s.r = r.u8()?;
    s.ei = r.u8()?; s.im = r.u8()?; s.p = r.u8()?; s.q = r.u8()?;
    s.iff1 = r.u8()? != 0;
    s.iff2 = r.u8()? != 0;
    let n = r.u8()? as usize;
    s.ram = Vec::with_capacity(n);
    for _ in 0..n {
        s.ram.push(RamEntry { addr: r.u16()?, val: r.u8()? });
    }
    Ok(s)
}
```

git commit -m "feat(testrunner): the Z80V vector format, writer and parser

Upstream ships 1.37 GB of JSON and this workspace has no JSON parser in
any shipping crate. This is what the converter writes instead: ~236 MB,
measured at 5.8x shrink across all seven opcode pages.

data_valid is an explicit bit rather than derived from the pins, because
upstream's r-m- samples carry no data at all (15,000 of 15,000) while
plain internal samples carry it a quarter of the time. A reader that
inferred validity from 'is this a read' would drop every byte the CPU
returned and pass against a core that fabricated all of them.

The writer's bounds are checked and panic rather than truncating. 5 RAM
entries against a 255 ceiling is not a fit worth worrying about; a
silent 'as u8' is how the format grows a bug when the suite is
regenerated with different options.

## Task 3: The JSON reader

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/testrunner/src/z80json.rs`
- Modify: `crates/testrunner/src/lib.rs` (add `pub mod z80json;`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `z80fmt::{Case, State, Cycle, Port, RamEntry}` from Task 2.
- Produces: `z80json::parse(text: &str) -> Result<Vec<Case>, JsonError>`, converting
  upstream JSON straight into the binary format's types. Task 4's fetcher is the
  only caller.

**The upstream shape**, which this reader handles and nothing else:

```json
[
  {
    "name": "00 0000",
    "initial": { "pc": 22437, "sp": 4361, "a": 143, "b": 5, "c": 33,
                 "d": 179, "e": 91, "f": 165, "h": 226, "l": 5,
                 "i": 22, "r": 118, "ix": 61425, "iy": 20946, "wz": 5678,
                 "af_": 4386, "bc_": 5678, "de_": 39030, "hl_": 65261,
                 "ei": 0, "im": 0, "p": 0, "q": 0, "iff1": 0, "iff2": 0,
                 "ram": [[22437, 0]] },
    "final":   { ... same 26 keys ... },
    "cycles":  [[22437, null, "----"], [22437, 0, "r-m-"]],
    "ports":   [[43982, 90, "w"]]
  }
]
```

`ports` is absent on non-I/O instructions. Every other key is always present —
verified: the key sets of `initial` and `final` are equal across the suite.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `crates/testrunner/src/z80json.rs` with tests first. The fixture below is
the upstream shape reproduced by hand — a *literal* in the test, not a downloaded
file, so this test passes on a machine that has never fetched anything:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    const ONE: &str = r#"[
      {
        "name": "00 0000",
        "initial": {"pc": 22437, "sp": 4361, "a": 143, "b": 5, "c": 33,
                    "d": 179, "e": 91, "f": 165, "h": 226, "l": 5,
                    "i": 22, "r": 118, "ix": 61425, "iy": 20946, "wz": 5678,
                    "af_": 4386, "bc_": 5678, "de_": 39030, "hl_": 65261,
                    "ei": 0, "im": 0, "p": 0, "q": 0, "iff1": 0, "iff2": 1,
                    "ram": [[22437, 0]]},
        "final": {"pc": 22438, "sp": 4361, "a": 143, "b": 5, "c": 33,
                  "d": 179, "e": 91, "f": 165, "h": 226, "l": 5,
                  "i": 22, "r": 119, "ix": 61425, "iy": 20946, "wz": 5678,
                  "af_": 4386, "bc_": 5678, "de_": 39030, "hl_": 65261,
                  "ei": 0, "im": 0, "p": 0, "q": 0, "iff1": 0, "iff2": 1,
                  "ram": [[22437, 0]]},
        "cycles": [[22437, null, "----"], [22437, 0, "r-m-"],
                   [22437, 0, "----"], [22437, null, "----"]]
      }
    ]"#;

    /// Every field of a case survives the conversion.
    ///
    /// Asserted field by field rather than against a `Case` literal, because a
    /// whole-struct comparison built from the same reading of the JSON would agree
    /// with a reader that transposed two keys. `r` is 118 in, 119 out — the M1
    /// refresh increment — and that asymmetry is what proves `initial` and `final`
    /// were not read from the same object.
    #[test]
    fn a_case_converts_field_by_field() {
        let cs = parse(ONE).expect("parses");
        assert_eq!(cs.len(), 1);
        let c = &cs[0];
        assert_eq!(c.initial.pc, 22437);
        assert_eq!(c.final_.pc, 22438, "final, not initial");
        assert_eq!(c.initial.r, 118);
        assert_eq!(c.final_.r, 119, "M1 bumped the refresh counter");
        assert_eq!(c.initial.i, 22);
        assert_eq!(c.initial.ix, 61425);
        assert_eq!(c.initial.iy, 20946);
        assert_eq!(c.initial.wz, 5678);
        assert_eq!(c.initial.af_, 4386);
        assert_eq!(c.initial.hl_, 65261);
        assert_eq!((c.initial.a, c.initial.f), (143, 165));
        assert_eq!((c.initial.b, c.initial.c), (5, 33));
        assert_eq!((c.initial.d, c.initial.e), (179, 91));
        assert_eq!((c.initial.h, c.initial.l), (226, 5));
        assert!(!c.initial.iff1, "0 is false");
        assert!(c.initial.iff2, "1 is true, and they differ here on purpose");
        assert_eq!(c.initial.ram, vec![RamEntry { addr: 22437, val: 0 }]);
        assert!(c.ports.is_empty(), "absent ports is empty, not an error");
    }

    /// The pin string becomes four booleans, and `null` data becomes `None`.
    ///
    /// The third sample carries data with **no pins set** — that is the format's
    /// trap, and it is asserted here rather than described.
    #[test]
    fn the_pin_string_becomes_pins_and_null_becomes_none() {
        let c = &parse(ONE).expect("parses")[0];
        assert_eq!(c.cycles.len(), 4, "NOP is 4 T-states");
        assert_eq!(c.cycles[0].data, None);
        assert!(!c.cycles[0].rd && !c.cycles[0].mreq, "---- is internal");
        assert!(c.cycles[1].rd && c.cycles[1].mreq, "r-m- is a read request");
        assert_eq!(c.cycles[1].data, None, "and it carries no byte");
        assert_eq!(c.cycles[2].data, Some(0), "the byte lands on a later sample");
        assert!(!c.cycles[2].rd, "with no pins asserted at all");
    }

    /// The four pin patterns the suite uses, each decoded.
    #[test]
    fn all_four_pin_patterns_decode() {
        for (s, want) in [
            ("----", (false, false, false, false)),
            ("r-m-", (true, false, true, false)),
            ("-wm-", (false, true, true, false)),
            ("r--i", (true, false, false, true)),
            ("-w-i", (false, true, false, true)),
        ] {
            let got = pins(s).unwrap_or_else(|_| panic!("{s} must decode"));
            assert_eq!(got, want, "pins {s:?}");
        }
    }

    /// An unrecognised pin string is an error naming it, never a default.
    ///
    /// The suite could grow a pattern — BUSACK, or a HALT indication — and a reader
    /// that silently decoded the unknown as "no pins" would compare a core against
    /// a trace it had misread, and pass.
    #[test]
    fn an_unknown_pin_string_is_a_loud_error() {
        let e = pins("rwmi").expect_err("must reject");
        assert!(e.to_string().contains("rwmi"), "{e}");
        assert!(pins("--").is_err(), "wrong length is wrong");
        assert!(pins("").is_err());
    }

    /// A port transaction's direction comes from the token, and only two are known.
    #[test]
    fn a_port_direction_is_r_or_w_and_nothing_else() {
        let txt = ONE.replace(
            r#""cycles":"#,
            r#""ports": [[43982, 90, "w"], [4660, 7, "r"]], "cycles":"#,
        );
        let c = &parse(&txt).expect("parses")[0];
        assert_eq!(c.ports.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(c.ports[0], Port { addr: 43982, val: 90, out: true });
        assert_eq!(c.ports[1], Port { addr: 4660, val: 7, out: false });

        let bad = ONE.replace(
            r#""cycles":"#,
            r#""ports": [[1, 2, "x"]], "cycles":"#,
        );
        assert!(parse(&bad).is_err(), "an unknown direction is an error");
    }

    /// A missing key is an error naming the key.
    ///
    /// One test per key would be 26 near-identical tests; this removes each in turn
    /// from the same fixture, which also means a key added later is covered without
    /// editing the test.
    #[test]
    fn every_missing_state_key_is_an_error_naming_it() {
        for key in [
            "pc", "sp", "a", "b", "c", "d", "e", "f", "h", "l", "i", "r", "ix",
            "iy", "wz", "af_", "bc_", "de_", "hl_", "ei", "im", "p", "q",
            "iff1", "iff2", "ram",
        ] {
            let broken = ONE.replacen(&format!("\"{key}\""), "\"zz_removed\"", 1);
            let e = parse(&broken)
                .unwrap_or_else(|_| panic!("must reject a missing {key}"))
                .len();
            let _ = e;
        }
    }
}
```

**Note on that last test as written above:** `unwrap_or_else` on a `Result` whose
`Ok` is a `Vec` cannot express "must be an error". Write it as:

```rust
    #[test]
    fn every_missing_state_key_is_an_error_naming_it() {
        for key in [
            "pc", "sp", "a", "b", "c", "d", "e", "f", "h", "l", "i", "r", "ix",
            "iy", "wz", "af_", "bc_", "de_", "hl_", "ei", "im", "p", "q",
            "iff1", "iff2", "ram",
        ] {
            let broken = ONE.replacen(&format!("\"{key}\""), "\"zz_removed\"", 1);
            let e = match parse(&broken) {
                Err(e) => e,
                Ok(_) => panic!("a case missing {key:?} must not parse"),
            };
            assert!(e.to_string().contains(key), "error must name {key}: {e}");
        }
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the reader**

A hand-written scanner, not a serde dependency. It exploits the shape rather than
parsing general JSON: find a key, read the value after it. That is enough for one
known generator's output and it is 200 lines instead of a dependency.

```rust
//! A reader for the one JSON shape the Z80 vectors come in.
//!
//! Not a general JSON parser and not trying to be. `testrunner` is dev-only, but
//! adding `serde_json` here would put it in the workspace lock file and in every
//! contributor's build, to read a format that is generated by one program and
//! never hand-edited. So this scans for the keys it needs and rejects anything it
//! does not recognise — loudly, by name, because a reader that guessed would
//! compare the core against a trace it had misread and report success.

use crate::z80fmt::{Case, Cycle, Port, RamEntry, State};

/// What can be wrong with a vector JSON file.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct JsonError(String);

impl core::fmt::Display for JsonError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut core::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> core::fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(&self.0)
    }
}

impl std::error::Error for JsonError {}

fn err<T>(msg: impl Into<String>) -> Result<T, JsonError> {
    Err(JsonError(msg.into()))
}

/// Decodes a pin string into `(rd, wr, mreq, ioreq)`.
///
/// The suite writes four characters: `r` or `-`, `w` or `-`, `m` or `-`, `i` or
/// `-`. Any other string is an error — see the test for why a default would be
/// worse than a failure.
pub fn pins(s: &str) -> Result<(bool, bool, bool, bool), JsonError> {
    let b = s.as_bytes();
    if b.len() != 4 {
        return err(format!("pin string {s:?} is not four characters"));
    }
    let bit = |i: usize, set: u8| -> Result<bool, JsonError> {
        match b[i] {
            c if c == set => Ok(true),
            b'-' => Ok(false),
            _ => err(format!("pin string {s:?} has an unknown character at {i}")),
        }
    };
    Ok((bit(0, b'r')?, bit(1, b'w')?, bit(2, b'm')?, bit(3, b'i')?))
}
```

The scanner itself, appended to the same file:

```rust
/// A cursor over the JSON text.
struct Sc<'a> {
    s: &'a str,
    at: usize,
}

impl<'a> Sc<'a> {
    fn ws(&mut self) {
        while self.at < self.s.len() && self.s.as_bytes()[self.at].is_ascii_whitespace() {
            self.at += 1;
        }
    }
    fn eat(&mut self, c: u8) -> Result<(), JsonError> {
        self.ws();
        if self.s.as_bytes().get(self.at) == Some(&c) {
            self.at += 1;
            Ok(())
        } else {
            err(format!("expected {:?} at byte {}", c as char, self.at))
        }
    }
    fn peek(&mut self) -> Option<u8> {
        self.ws();
        self.s.as_bytes().get(self.at).copied()
    }
    /// Reads a `"quoted"` string. The suite's strings have no escapes.
    fn string(&mut self) -> Result<&'a str, JsonError> {
        self.eat(b'"')?;
        let start = self.at;
        while self.at < self.s.len() && self.s.as_bytes()[self.at] != b'"' {
            if self.s.as_bytes()[self.at] == b'\\' {
                return err(format!("escape at byte {} is not supported", self.at));
            }
            self.at += 1;
        }
        if self.at >= self.s.len() {
            return err("unterminated string".to_string());
        }
        let out = &self.s[start..self.at];
        self.at += 1;
        Ok(out)
    }
    /// Reads a non-negative integer or `null`. `None` is `null`.
    fn num(&mut self) -> Result<Option<u32>, JsonError> {
        self.ws();
        if self.s[self.at..].starts_with("null") {
            self.at += 4;
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let start = self.at;
        while self.at < self.s.len() && self.s.as_bytes()[self.at].is_ascii_digit() {
            self.at += 1;
        }
        if start == self.at {
            return err(format!("expected a number at byte {start}"));
        }
        self.s[start..self.at]
            .parse::<u32>()
            .map(Some)
            .map_err(|e| JsonError(format!("bad number at byte {start}: {e}")))
    }
}
```

State and case reading:

```rust
/// Reads one `[a, b, c]`-style array of numbers, of a known length.
fn tuple<const N: usize>(sc: &mut Sc) -> Result<[Option<u32>; N], JsonError> {
    sc.eat(b'[')?;
    let mut out = [None; N];
    for (i, slot) in out.iter_mut().enumerate() {
        if i > 0 {
            sc.eat(b',')?;
        }
        *slot = sc.num()?;
    }
    sc.eat(b']')?;
    Ok(out)
}

fn need(v: Option<u32>, what: &str) -> Result<u32, JsonError> {
    v.ok_or_else(|| JsonError(format!("{what} must not be null")))
}

/// Reads a state object: all 26 keys, in whatever order they appear.
fn state(sc: &mut Sc) -> Result<State, JsonError> {
    let mut s = State::default();
    let mut seen = 0u32;
    sc.eat(b'{')?;
    loop {
        let key = sc.string()?;
        sc.eat(b':')?;
        let mut mark = |b: u32| seen |= 1 << b;
        match key {
            "ram" => {
                mark(0);
                sc.eat(b'[')?;
                while sc.peek() == Some(b'[') {
                    let [a, v] = tuple::<2>(sc)?;
                    s.ram.push(RamEntry {
                        addr: u16::try_from(need(a, "ram address")?)
                            .map_err(|e| JsonError(format!("ram address: {e}")))?,
                        val: u8::try_from(need(v, "ram value")?)
                            .map_err(|e| JsonError(format!("ram value: {e}")))?,
                    });
                    if sc.peek() == Some(b',') {
                        sc.eat(b',')?;
                    }
                }
                sc.eat(b']')?;
            }
            _ => {
                let n = need(sc.num()?, key)?;
                let as16 = |n: u32| -> Result<u16, JsonError> {
                    u16::try_from(n).map_err(|_| JsonError(format!("{key} = {n} exceeds u16")))
                };
                let as8 = |n: u32| -> Result<u8, JsonError> {
                    u8::try_from(n).map_err(|_| JsonError(format!("{key} = {n} exceeds u8")))
                };
                match key {
                    "pc" => { mark(1); s.pc = as16(n)? }
                    "sp" => { mark(2); s.sp = as16(n)? }
                    "ix" => { mark(3); s.ix = as16(n)? }
                    "iy" => { mark(4); s.iy = as16(n)? }
                    "wz" => { mark(5); s.wz = as16(n)? }
                    "af_" => { mark(6); s.af_ = as16(n)? }
                    "bc_" => { mark(7); s.bc_ = as16(n)? }
                    "de_" => { mark(8); s.de_ = as16(n)? }
                    "hl_" => { mark(9); s.hl_ = as16(n)? }
                    "a" => { mark(10); s.a = as8(n)? }
                    "b" => { mark(11); s.b = as8(n)? }
                    "c" => { mark(12); s.c = as8(n)? }
                    "d" => { mark(13); s.d = as8(n)? }
                    "e" => { mark(14); s.e = as8(n)? }
                    "f" => { mark(15); s.f = as8(n)? }
                    "h" => { mark(16); s.h = as8(n)? }
                    "l" => { mark(17); s.l = as8(n)? }
                    "i" => { mark(18); s.i = as8(n)? }
                    "r" => { mark(19); s.r = as8(n)? }
                    "ei" => { mark(20); s.ei = as8(n)? }
                    "im" => { mark(21); s.im = as8(n)? }
                    "p" => { mark(22); s.p = as8(n)? }
                    "q" => { mark(23); s.q = as8(n)? }
                    "iff1" => { mark(24); s.iff1 = n != 0 }
                    "iff2" => { mark(25); s.iff2 = n != 0 }
                    other => return err(format!("unknown state key {other:?}")),
                }
            }
        }
        if sc.peek() == Some(b',') {
            sc.eat(b',')?;
        } else {
            break;
        }
    }
    sc.eat(b'}')?;
    const KEYS: [&str; 26] = [
        "ram", "pc", "sp", "ix", "iy", "wz", "af_", "bc_", "de_", "hl_", "a", "b",
        "c", "d", "e", "f", "h", "l", "i", "r", "ei", "im", "p", "q", "iff1", "iff2",
    ];
    for (b, name) in KEYS.iter().enumerate() {
        if seen & (1 << b) == 0 {
            return err(format!("state is missing {name:?}"));
        }
    }
    Ok(s)
}

/// Parses a whole vector JSON file into cases.
pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Vec<Case>, JsonError> {
    let sc = &mut Sc { s: text, at: 0 };
    sc.eat(b'[')?;
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    while sc.peek() == Some(b'{') {
        out.push(case(sc)?);
        if sc.peek() == Some(b',') {
            sc.eat(b',')?;
        }
    }
    sc.eat(b']')?;
    Ok(out)
}

fn case(sc: &mut Sc) -> Result<Case, JsonError> {
    let mut c = Case::default();
    let (mut got_i, mut got_f, mut got_c) = (false, false, false);
    sc.eat(b'{')?;
    loop {
        let key = sc.string()?;
        sc.eat(b':')?;
        match key {
            // Dropped: recoverable from the filename and the case index.
            "name" => {
                sc.string()?;
            }
            "initial" => {
                c.initial = state(sc)?;
                got_i = true;
            }
            "final" => {
                c.final_ = state(sc)?;
                got_f = true;
            }
            "cycles" => {
                sc.eat(b'[')?;
                while sc.peek() == Some(b'[') {
                    sc.eat(b'[')?;
                    let addr = need(sc.num()?, "cycle address")?;
                    sc.eat(b',')?;
                    let data = sc.num()?;
                    sc.eat(b',')?;
                    let p = sc.string()?;
                    sc.eat(b']')?;
                    let (rd, wr, mreq, ioreq) = pins(p)?;
                    c.cycles.push(Cycle {
                        addr: u16::try_from(addr)
                            .map_err(|_| JsonError(format!("cycle address {addr} exceeds u16")))?,
                        data: match data {
                            None => None,
                            Some(d) => Some(
                                u8::try_from(d)
                                    .map_err(|_| JsonError(format!("cycle data {d} exceeds u8")))?,
                            ),
                        },
                        rd,
                        wr,
                        mreq,
                        ioreq,
                    });
                    if sc.peek() == Some(b',') {
                        sc.eat(b',')?;
                    }
                }
                sc.eat(b']')?;
                got_c = true;
            }
            "ports" => {
                sc.eat(b'[')?;
                while sc.peek() == Some(b'[') {
                    sc.eat(b'[')?;
                    let addr = need(sc.num()?, "port address")?;
                    sc.eat(b',')?;
                    let val = need(sc.num()?, "port value")?;
                    sc.eat(b',')?;
                    let dir = sc.string()?;
                    sc.eat(b']')?;
                    c.ports.push(Port {
                        addr: u16::try_from(addr)
                            .map_err(|_| JsonError(format!("port address {addr} exceeds u16")))?,
                        val: u8::try_from(val)
                            .map_err(|_| JsonError(format!("port value {val} exceeds u8")))?,
                        out: match dir {
                            "w" => true,
                            "r" => false,
                            o => return err(format!("unknown port direction {o:?}")),
                        },
                    });
                    if sc.peek() == Some(b',') {
                        sc.eat(b',')?;
                    }
                }
                sc.eat(b']')?;
            }
            other => return err(format!("unknown case key {other:?}")),
        }
        if sc.peek() == Some(b',') {
            sc.eat(b',')?;
        } else {
            break;
        }
    }
    sc.eat(b'}')?;
    for (ok, what) in [(got_i, "initial"), (got_f, "final"), (got_c, "cycles")] {
        if !ok {
            return err(format!("case is missing {what:?}"));
        }
    }
    Ok(c)
}
```

---

## Task 4: The streaming fetcher

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/testrunner/src/z80files.rs`
- Create: `crates/testrunner/src/bin/fetchz80.rs`
- Modify: `crates/testrunner/src/lib.rs` (add `pub mod z80files;`)
- Modify: `.gitignore` — confirm `testdata/` already covers `testdata/z80/`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `z80json::parse`, `z80fmt::write_file`.
- Produces: `z80files::{all_names, Page, page_of, EXPECTED, page_counts}` — the
  authoritative file list, used by the fetcher *and* by Task 6's completeness
  test. `z80files::stem(name)` gives the on-disk stem (`"dd cb __ 06"` →
  `"dd_cb____06"`), so the suite and the fetcher agree on filenames.

### Why the file list is derived, and asserted

1,604 names are not typed out. They follow rules, and the rules are checkable:

- **252 plain**: `00`–`ff` less the four prefix bytes `cb`, `dd`, `ed`, `fd`.
- **256 `cb NN`**: the whole page.
- **252 `dd NN`** and **252 `fd NN`**: `00`–`ff` less the same four prefix bytes
  (`dd cb` is a page of its own; `dd dd`/`dd ed`/`dd fd` re-prefix).
- **80 `ed NN`**: `40`–`7f` in full (64), plus the block instructions `a0`–`a3`,
  `a8`–`ab`, `b0`–`b3`, `b8`–`bb` (16). The rest of the `ed` page is undefined and
  upstream ships no file for it.
- **256 `dd cb __ NN`** and **256 `fd cb __ NN`**: the whole double-prefix pages,
  with a literal `__` where the displacement byte goes.

252+256+252+252+80+256+256 = **1,604**, and `all_names_matches_the_upstream_inventory`
asserts that total plus each page's count as literals. A derivation that is not
pinned to the measured inventory is a guess with extra steps.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests for the file list**

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// The derived list matches the inventory measured upstream on 2026-08-08.
    ///
    /// The totals are literals. The generation rules could each be wrong in a way
    /// that still produces a plausible list, and the only thing that catches that
    /// is the count the suite actually has.
    #[test]
    fn all_names_matches_the_upstream_inventory() {
        let n = all_names();
        assert_eq!(n.len(), 1604, "the suite has 1,604 files");
        assert_eq!(n.len(), EXPECTED, "and EXPECTED must say so too");
        let c = page_counts(&n);
        assert_eq!(c[Page::Base as usize], 252, "00-ff less the four prefixes");
        assert_eq!(c[Page::Cb as usize], 256);
        assert_eq!(c[Page::Ed as usize], 80, "only 40-7f and the block forms");
        assert_eq!(c[Page::Dd as usize], 252);
        assert_eq!(c[Page::Fd as usize], 252);
        assert_eq!(c[Page::DdCb as usize], 256);
        assert_eq!(c[Page::FdCb as usize], 256);
    }

    /// The four prefix bytes have no file of their own on the pages that omit them.
    #[test]
    fn a_prefix_byte_is_not_an_instruction_file() {
        let n = all_names();
        for absent in ["cb", "dd", "ed", "fd"] {
            assert!(!n.contains(&absent.to_string()), "{absent} is a prefix");
            for p in ["dd", "fd"] {
                let s = format!("{p} {absent}");
                assert!(!n.contains(&s), "{s} is a prefix or its own page");
            }
        }
        // But the pages they open are all there.
        assert!(n.contains(&"cb 00".to_string()));
        assert!(n.contains(&"ed 40".to_string()));
        assert!(n.contains(&"dd cb __ 06".to_string()));
        assert!(n.contains(&"fd cb __ ff".to_string()));
    }

    /// The `ed` page holds exactly the defined opcodes and no others.
    ///
    /// Spot-checked at the boundaries, because an off-by-one in a range is what
    /// this rule is most likely to get wrong and the count alone would not catch
    /// a range shifted by one in both directions.
    #[test]
    fn the_ed_page_is_the_defined_opcodes_only() {
        let n = all_names();
        let has = |s: &str| n.contains(&s.to_string());
        assert!(has("ed 40") && has("ed 7f"), "the 40-7f block is whole");
        assert!(!has("ed 3f") && !has("ed 80"), "and it stops at both ends");
        assert!(has("ed a0") && has("ed a3"), "LDI CPI INI OUTI");
        assert!(!has("ed a4"), "and nothing after them");
        assert!(has("ed b8") && has("ed bb"), "LDDR CPDR INDR OTDR");
        assert!(!has("ed bc") && !has("ed 9f"), "nor around them");
    }

    /// A name maps to a filesystem stem with no spaces.
    ///
    /// Spaces in paths work but invite quoting bugs in every shell command that
    /// touches `testdata/`, and the `__` displacement placeholder must survive
    /// unmangled so a stem still reads as its opcode.
    #[test]
    fn a_stem_replaces_spaces_and_keeps_the_displacement_marker() {
        assert_eq!(stem("00"), "00");
        assert_eq!(stem("cb 06"), "cb_06");
        assert_eq!(stem("dd cb __ 06"), "dd_cb____06");
        // Distinct names must give distinct stems, or two files collide silently.
        let n = all_names();
        let mut stems: Vec<String> = n.iter().map(|s| stem(s)).collect();
        stems.sort();
        let before = stems.len();
        stems.dedup();
        assert_eq!(stems.len(), before, "stems must be unique");
    }

    /// Every name is claimed by exactly one page.
    #[test]
    fn page_of_partitions_the_list() {
        for name in all_names() {
            let p = page_of(&name).unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name} has no page"));
            // And the page's own prefix test agrees.
            assert!(p.claims(&name), "{name} vs {p:?}");
            for other in Page::ALL {
                if other != p {
                    assert!(!other.claims(&name), "{name} claimed by {p:?} and {other:?}");
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `z80files.rs`**

```rust
//! The Z80 vector suite's file inventory.
//!
//! Derived from the naming rules rather than typed out, and then checked against
//! the counts measured upstream on 2026-08-08. Both halves matter: the rules keep
//! it to 40 lines, and the counts catch a rule that is plausible and wrong.

/// The number of vector files the suite has.
pub const EXPECTED: usize = 1604;

/// The bytes that open a page instead of being an instruction.
const PREFIXES: [u8; 4] = [0xCB, 0xDD, 0xED, 0xFD];

/// Which opcode page a file belongs to.
#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
pub enum Page {
    Base,
    Cb,
    Ed,
    Dd,
    Fd,
    DdCb,
    FdCb,
}

impl Page {
    /// Every page, in the order their discriminants are used as indices.
    pub const ALL: [Page; 7] =
        [Page::Base, Page::Cb, Page::Ed, Page::Dd, Page::Fd, Page::DdCb, Page::FdCb];

    /// Whether this page claims `name`.
    ///
    /// Order matters: `dd cb __ 06` starts with `dd `, so the double-prefix pages
    /// must be tested before the single ones. That precedence is why this is one
    /// function rather than seven independent predicates.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn claims(self, name: &str) -> bool {
        match self {
            Page::DdCb => name.starts_with("dd cb "),
            Page::FdCb => name.starts_with("fd cb "),
            Page::Cb => name.starts_with("cb "),
            Page::Ed => name.starts_with("ed "),
            Page::Dd => name.starts_with("dd ") && !name.starts_with("dd cb "),
            Page::Fd => name.starts_with("fd ") && !name.starts_with("fd cb "),
            Page::Base => !name.contains(' '),
        }
    }
}

/// The page a name belongs to, or `None` if no page claims it.
#[must_use]
pub fn page_of(name: &str) -> Option<Page> {
    Page::ALL.into_iter().find(|p| p.claims(name))
}

/// How many names fall on each page, indexed by `Page`'s discriminant.
#[must_use]
pub fn page_counts(names: &[String]) -> [usize; 7] {
    let mut c = [0; 7];
    for n in names {
        if let Some(p) = page_of(n) {
            c[p as usize] += 1;
        }
    }
    c
}

/// The on-disk stem for a vector name: spaces become underscores.
///
/// `dd cb __ 06` becomes `dd_cb____06` — four underscores between `cb` and `06`,
/// because the placeholder's own two are kept and the two spaces around it are
/// converted as well. It looks odd and it round-trips, which is what matters.
#[must_use]
pub fn stem(name: &str) -> String {
    name.replace(' ', "_")
}

/// Every vector file's upstream name, in a stable order.
#[must_use]
pub fn all_names() -> Vec<String> {
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(EXPECTED);
    let plain = |b: u8| !PREFIXES.contains(&b);
    for op in 0u8..=0xFF {
        if plain(op) {
            out.push(format!("{op:02x}"));
        }
    }
    for op in 0u8..=0xFF {
        out.push(format!("cb {op:02x}"));
    }
    // The `ed` page is sparse: 40-7f entire, plus the four block-instruction
    // quartets. Everything else on the page is undefined and unshipped.
    for op in 0x40u8..=0x7F {
        out.push(format!("ed {op:02x}"));
    }
    for base in [0xA0u8, 0xA8, 0xB0, 0xB8] {
        for op in base..base + 4 {
            out.push(format!("ed {op:02x}"));
        }
    }
    for p in ["dd", "fd"] {
        for op in 0u8..=0xFF {
            if plain(op) {
                out.push(format!("{p} {op:02x}"));
            }
        }
    }
    for p in ["dd", "fd"] {
        for op in 0u8..=0xFF {
            out.push(format!("{p} cb __ {op:02x}"));
        }
    }
    out
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the fetcher**

`crates/testrunner/src/bin/fetchz80.rs`. The resumable pattern is copied from
`fetch.rs`: skip an existing output, download to `.part`, rename on success.

```rust
//! Downloads the Z80 vector suite and converts it as it goes.
//!
//! Upstream is 1.37 GB of JSON and this machine has under 3 GiB free, so nothing
//! is kept: each file is downloaded to a temporary path, converted to the
//! `Z80V` binary form (about 5.8x smaller), and the JSON deleted before the next
//! one starts. Peak extra disk is one JSON file plus the growing output — about
//! 236 MB when it finishes.
//!
//! Resumable, because 1,604 sequential downloads will be interrupted: a file whose
//! `.z80bin` already exists is skipped, and output is written to `.part` and
//! renamed only after a successful conversion, so an interrupted run leaves no
//! half-file for the suite to read.
//!
//! ```text
//! cargo run -p testrunner --bin fetchz80
//! ```
//!
//! No ROM is involved. The vectors are MIT-licensed CPU test data from
//! SingleStepTests/z80; nothing here downloads game code.

use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::Command;
use testrunner::{z80files, z80fmt, z80json};

const BASE: &str = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SingleStepTests/z80/main/v1";

fn main() {
    let out = PathBuf::from("testdata/z80");
    std::fs::create_dir_all(&out).expect("create testdata/z80");
    let names = z80files::all_names();
    assert_eq!(names.len(), z80files::EXPECTED, "inventory changed");

    let (mut done, mut fetched) = (0usize, 0usize);
    for (i, name) in names.iter().enumerate() {
        let stem = z80files::stem(name);
        let dest = out.join(format!("{stem}.z80bin"));
        if dest.exists() {
            done += 1;
            continue;
        }
        let tmp_json = out.join(format!("{stem}.json.part"));
        let tmp_bin = out.join(format!("{stem}.z80bin.part"));

        // Spaces and the `__` marker both need percent-encoding decisions; the
        // only one that matters is the space, and curl will not do it for us.
        let url = format!("{BASE}/{}.json", name.replace(' ', "%20"));
        let st = Command::new("curl")
            .args(["-sfL", "--retry", "3", "-o"])
            .arg(&tmp_json)
            .arg(&url)
            .status()
            .expect("run curl");
        assert!(st.success(), "curl failed for {name:?} ({url})");

        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&tmp_json)
            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("read {}: {e}", tmp_json.display()));
        let cases = z80json::parse(&text)
            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("parse {name:?}: {e}"));
        assert_eq!(cases.len(), 1000, "{name:?} should have 1,000 cases");
        std::fs::write(&tmp_bin, z80fmt::write_file(&cases))
            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("write {}: {e}", tmp_bin.display()));
        std::fs::rename(&tmp_bin, &dest).expect("rename into place");
        // The JSON goes now, not at the end: keeping 1,604 of them is the thing
        // this program exists to avoid.
        std::fs::remove_file(&tmp_json).expect("remove the JSON");

        fetched += 1;
        if fetched % 50 == 0 {
            println!("{}/{} ({} already present)", i + 1, names.len(), done);
        }
    }
    println!("z80 vectors: {fetched} fetched, {done} already present");
}
```

```bash
cargo fmt --all && cargo fmt --all --check
cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
cargo test --workspace && cargo test --workspace --release
cargo doc --no-deps --workspace
git add crates/testrunner
git commit -m "feat(testrunner): stream-fetch and convert the Z80 vectors

1,604 files, 1.37 GB of JSON, on a machine with under 3 GiB free. So
nothing is kept: download one, convert it, delete the JSON, continue.
Output is ~236 MB.

The file list is derived from the naming rules and then checked against
the counts measured upstream -- 252/256/80/252/252/256/256. The counts
are literals because a rule can be plausible and wrong, and the ed
page's sparseness (40-7f plus the block quartets, nothing else) is
exactly the kind of rule that is.

Resumable: an existing .z80bin is skipped, output lands via .part, so an
interrupted run leaves nothing half-written for the suite to read.

No ROM is fetched. These are MIT-licensed CPU vectors.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
```

## Task 5: Flags, the M1 fetch, and `step`

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/z80/src/flags.rs`
- Create: `crates/z80/src/decode.rs`
- Modify: `crates/z80/src/cpu.rs` (add `step`, `fetch`, `bump_r`)
- Modify: `crates/z80/src/lib.rs` (register the modules)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Z80`, `Bus` (Task 1).
- Produces:
  - `flags::{S, Z, F5, H, F3, PV, N, C}` — the eight flag-bit constants.
  - `flags::parity(v: u8) -> bool`, `flags::sz53(v: u8) -> u8`,
    `flags::sz53p(v: u8) -> u8`.
  - `Z80::step<B: Bus>(&mut self, bus: &mut B) -> u32` — executes one
    instruction, returns T-states. **This signature is what Task 6's `run_case`
    calls and every later task extends.**
  - `Z80::fetch<B: Bus>(&mut self, bus: &mut B) -> u8` — an M1 opcode fetch: read
    at `PC`, increment `PC`, bump `R`.
  - `decode::execute<B: Bus>(cpu: &mut Z80, bus: &mut B, op: u8) -> u32` — the base
    page's dispatch. Tasks 7–9 fill its arms; this task implements `NOP`, `HALT`,
    `DI`, `EI`, `SCF`, `CCF`, `CPL`, and `DAA`.

### The flag bits, and which two are the trap

| bit | 7 | 6 | 5 | 4 | 3 | 2 | 1 | 0 |
|-----|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| name | S | Z | F5 | H | F3 | P/V | N | C |

F5 and F3 are **undocumented** and hold copies of result bits 5 and 3 for most
instructions. They are not optional here: they are part of `f`, and `f` is compared
on all 1,604,000 cases.

`SCF` and `CCF` are the special case. They set F3/F5 from `A` if the previous
instruction wrote the flags, and **OR** them with the previous flags if it did not.
That is what `q` is for, and getting it wrong is the single most common Z80 core
defect.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing flag tests**

Create `crates/z80/src/flags.rs`, tests first. **Every expectation below is
hand-computed from the Zilog manual and the flag algebra** — none is read off a
vector file, which would be an assertion that cannot fail:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    /// Parity is even parity: set when the number of set bits is even.
    ///
    /// Computed by hand at values chosen for their bit counts, not by comparing
    /// against a second implementation of the same idea.
    #[test]
    fn parity_is_set_when_the_bit_count_is_even() {
        assert!(parity(0x00), "zero bits set is even");
        assert!(!parity(0x01), "one bit");
        assert!(parity(0x03), "two bits");
        assert!(!parity(0x07), "three bits");
        assert!(parity(0xFF), "eight bits");
        assert!(!parity(0x7F), "seven bits");
        assert!(parity(0x81), "two bits, far apart");
        assert!(!parity(0x80), "one bit, high");
    }

    /// `sz53` takes S, Z, and the two undocumented bits from a result.
    ///
    /// The values are written out in binary so the four bits being claimed are
    /// visible in the test rather than implied by a mask.
    #[test]
    fn sz53_copies_sign_zero_and_bits_five_and_three() {
        assert_eq!(sz53(0x00), Z, "zero sets Z and nothing else");
        assert_eq!(sz53(0x80), S, "bit 7 is the sign");
        assert_eq!(sz53(0x28), F5 | F3, "bits 5 and 3 are copied straight");
        assert_eq!(sz53(0xA8), S | F5 | F3);
        assert_eq!(sz53(0x01), 0, "bit 0 touches no flag here");
        // H, N, C and P/V are never set by sz53 -- they are the caller's business.
        for v in [0x00u8, 0x01, 0x7F, 0x80, 0xFF] {
            assert_eq!(sz53(v) & (H | N | C | PV), 0, "sz53({v:#04X}) must not set H/N/C/PV");
        }
    }

    /// `sz53p` is `sz53` plus even parity in the P/V bit.
    #[test]
    fn sz53p_adds_parity() {
        assert_eq!(sz53p(0x00), Z | PV, "zero is even parity");
        assert_eq!(sz53p(0x01), 0, "one bit: odd, no parity, no sign");
        assert_eq!(sz53p(0xFF), S | F5 | F3 | PV, "eight bits, and 5 and 3 are set");
        assert_eq!(sz53p(0x80), S, "one bit, high: sign only");
    }

    /// The bit values themselves, because a transposed constant is invisible.
    ///
    /// `F5` at bit 5 and `F3` at bit 3 are the two that get swapped, and every
    /// other test in this file would pass with them swapped, since both are copied
    /// from bits of the same name.
    #[test]
    fn the_flag_bits_are_where_the_hardware_puts_them() {
        assert_eq!(S, 0x80);
        assert_eq!(Z, 0x40);
        assert_eq!(F5, 0x20);
        assert_eq!(H, 0x10);
        assert_eq!(F3, 0x08);
        assert_eq!(PV, 0x04);
        assert_eq!(N, 0x02);
        assert_eq!(C, 0x01);
    }
}
```
